October 2026\n}}\n\nThe '''2026–27 Premier League''' is the 35th season of the [[Premier League]] and the 128th season of {{nowrap|top-flight}} [[English football]] overall. The season began on 21 August 2026 and is scheduled to conclude on 30 May 2027. The full list of fixtures was released at 10:00 BST on 19 June 2026. \n\nThe 2026–27 season consists of 33 weekend and five midweek rounds of matches.<ref name=\":0\">{{Cite web |date=21 November 2025 |title=Dates for 2026/27 Premier League season confirmed |url=https://www.premierleague.com/en/news/4468487/dates-for-202627-premier-league-season-confirmed |access-date=23 February 2026 |publisher=Premier League |language=en}}</ref> The summer transfer window opened on 15 June 2026 and closed at 23:00 BST on 1 September 2026. The winter transfer window will open on 1 January 2027 and close at 23:00 GMT on 1 February 2027.<ref>{{Cite web |date=22 May 2026 |title=When does summer 2026 Premier League transfer window open and close? |url=https://www.premierleague.com/en/news/4664145/when-does-summer-2026-premier-league-transfer-window-open-and-close/ |access-date=29 August 2026|publisher=Premier League |language=en}}</ref>\n\n[[Arsenal F.C.|Arsenal]] are the defending champions.<ref>{{Cite web |date=19 May 2026 |title=Arsenal win first Premier League title in 22 years after Man City draw |url=https://www.espn.co.uk/football/story/_/id/48813813/arsenal-win-premier-league-title-2026-manchester-city-bournemouth |access-date=21 August 2026 |website=www.espn.co.uk|language=en}}</ref>\n\n==Summary==\n===Developments===\nThis season saw the usual opening fixtures and final matchweek postponed by one week due to the [[2026 FIFA World Cup]] taking place during the off-season. The fixtures were published at 10:00 BST on 19 June 2026.<ref name=\":0\" /><ref>{{Cite web |date=18 June 2026 |title=When will the 2026/27 Premier League fixtures be released? |url=https://www.premierleague.com/en/news/4625358/when-are-the-202627-premier-league-fixtures-released |access-date=19 June 2026 |publisher=Premier League |language=en}}</ref>\n\nThe previous calendar of five international breaks during the season will be reduced to four, with a three week international break between late September and early October occurring for the first time.<ref>{{cite news |last=Colman |first=Jonty |date=9 July 2026 |title=What are the key dates for the 2026-27 football season? |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/football/articles/cd0m840p7d1o |access-date=17 August 2026 |work=BBC Sport}}</ref> During the late September–early October international break, it was confirmed by the Premier League that [[Manchester City F.C.|Manchester City]] were found guilty of a total of 114 charges related to breaching the Premier League's financial regulations between 2009 and 2018 – the club is currently appealing this verdict.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Ornstein |first=David |date=2026-09-25 |title=Manchester City found guilty on almost all Premier League charges relating to financial breaches |url=https://www.nytimes.com/athletic/7049721/2026/09/25/manchester-city-charges-verdict-premier-league/ |access-date=2026-09-25 |work=The New York Times |language=en-US |issn=0362-4331}}</ref>\n\nThe Premier League implemented a ban on [[Gambling advertising|gambling sponsors]] appearing on the front of match day shirts from the 2026–27 season.<ref>{{cite web |date=18 April 2025 |title=Premier League shirt sponsors: Who are they and why are so many gambling companies involved? |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/football/articles/cge1lp0734jo |access-date=5 April 2026 |website=BBC Sport}}</ref> This initiative aims to reduce the visibility of betting brands, following pressure regarding gambling normalisation, though betting companies can still sponsor sleeves, training kits, LED signs, and other club partnerships.<ref>{{cite news |last=Taylor |first=Louise |date=13 April 2023 |title=Premier League clubs ban gambling sponsors on front of shirts from 2026-27 |url=https://www.theguardian.com/football/2023/apr/13/premier-league-clubs-ban-gambling-sponsors-on-front-of-shirts-from-2026-27 |access-date=5 April 2026 |newspaper=The Guardian |issn=0261-3077}}</ref>\n\n==Teams==\nTwenty teams will compete in the league – the top seventeen teams from the previous season and the three teams promoted from the [[2025–26 EFL Championship|Championship]]. The promoted teams are [[Coventry City F.C.|Coventry City]], [[Ipswich Town F.C.|Ipswich Town]] and [[Hull City A.F.C.|Hull City]], returning to the top flight after absences of twenty-five, one and nine years respectively.<ref>{{cite web |last=Mashiter |first=Nick |date=17 April 2026 |title=Coventry City: Sky Blues back in the Premier League after a quarter of a century |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/football/articles/cy814339170o |access-date=17 April 2026 |website=BBC Sport}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/news/articles/cy82pyeyddzo |title=How '£100m boost' for Ipswich Town could help club and county|date=6 May 2026 |work=BBC News |access-date=8 May 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Bloom |first=Ben |date=23 May 2026 |title=McBurnie’s late winner sinks Middlesbrough and takes Hull into Premier League |url=https://www.theguardian.com/football/2026/may/23/hull-middlesbrough-championship-playoff-final-match-report |access-date=23 May 2026 |newspaper=The Guardian |issn=0261-3077}}</ref> The twenty-five years between Coventry City's [[2000–01 Coventry City F.C. season|previous Premier League season]] and this season was the longest absence for a previous Premier League club in the Premier League era to date. They replaced [[West Ham United F.C.|West Ham United]], [[Burnley F.C.|Burnley]] and [[Wolverhampton Wanderers F.C.|Wolverhampton Wanderers]], who were relegated to the [[2026–27 EFL Championship|Championship]] after fourteen, one and eight years in the top flight respectively.<ref>{{cite web |title=Burnley 0-1 Man City: Erling Haaland scores to put Citizens top and relegate Clarets |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/football/live/cnv895p099jt |access-date=22 April 2026 |website=BBC Sport}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Mashiter |first=Nick |date=20 April 2026 |title=Why Wolves' relegation had been coming after slow decline |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/football/articles/ckg3l2k81x5o |access-date=21 April 2026 |website=BBC Sport}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.beinsports.com/en-nz/football/premier-league/articles-video/second-half-rout-not-enough-as-west-ham-relegated-2026-05-24 |title=Second-half rout not enough as West Ham relegated |publisher=beIN Sports |date=24 May 2026}}</ref>\n\n===Stadiums and locations===\n{{location map+ |England |width=320 |float=right |caption=Locations of the 2026–27 Premier League teams football}}\n{{2026–27 in European football (UEFA)}}\n\n{{DEFAULTSORT:Premier League}}\n[[Category:2026–27 Premier League| ]]\n[[Category:Premier League seasons|2026–27]]\n[[Category:2026–27 in English men's football leagues|1]]\n[[Category:2026–27 in European men's top-tier association football leagues|England]]\n[[Category:Current association football seasons|England 1]]","2026 Hilux NPC":"{{Short description|2026 rugby union competition in New Zealand}}\n{{Use dmy dates|date=June 2026}}\n{{Use New Zealand English|date=June 2026}}\n{{Infobox rugby union season\n| name             = {{nowrap|2026 Hilux NPC}}\n| image            = <!-- Do not use File:NPC-Logo 50 Years.png here per WP:NFC#UUI #17 -->\n| imagesize        = 165px\n| caption          = The official emblem\n| countries        = {{flagicon|NZL}} [[New Zealand]]\n| date             = 30 July&nbsp;– 24 October 2026\n| champions        =\n| runnersup        =\n| promoted         =\n| relegated        =\n| matches          = 76\n| tries            = 705\n| top point scorer = {{nowrap|[[Rivez Reihana]] (Northland)}}<br />119 points\n| top try scorer   = {{nowrap|[[Kyren Taumoefolau]] (Tasman)}}<br />19 tries\n| website          = https://www.provincial.rugby/\n| prevseason       = [[2025 Bunnings NPC|2025]]\n| nextseason       = [[2027 National Provincial Championship|2027]]\n}}\n\nThe '''2026 Hilux NPC''' is the twenty-first season of [[New Zealand]]'s provincial [[rugby union]] competition, the [[National Provincial Championship (2006–present)|National Provincial Championship]], since it turned professional in 2006. The 2026 NPC season also marks 50 years of provincial rugby in New Zealand since the competition's inception in 1976. The competition involves the top fourteen provincial rugby unions of New Zealand. The regular season began on 30 July 2026, when {{rut|Waikato}} hosted {{Rut|Bay of Plenty}}. The final is scheduled for 24 October 2026.\n\nThe competition saw much increased attendance, with over 300,000 fans passing through the gates during the regular season, a 57% increase on the previous season. Broadcast and streaming figures confirmed the growth in popularity of the NPC.<ref>{{cite news |url=https://www.rugbypass.com/news/hilux-npc-scores-big-wins-in-2026-match-attendance-and-social-media-engagement/ |title=Hilux NPC scores big wins in 2026 match attendance and social media engagement |work=RugbyPass |date=7 October 2026 |access-date=7 October 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite press release |url=https://www.provincial.rugby/news/fans-celebrate-record-breaking-hilux-npc-season |title=Fans celebrate record-breaking Hilux NPC season |website=allblacks.com |date=7 October 2026 |access-date=9 October 2026}}</ref>\n\n==Format==\nUnder the current competition format, which is largely a continuation of that of [[2023 Bunnings NPC|2023]], the 14 provincial unions participating in the [[National Provincial Championship (2006–present)|National Provincial Championship]] (NPC) are grouped in one single division, ranked on one single competition table and play for one NPC title.<ref name=\"Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026\">{{Cite web | url=https://www.nzrugby.co.nz/assets/2026-Competition-Regulations-Handbook.pdf | title=Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026 | work=New Zealand Rugby Union | year=2026 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref><ref name=\"About the National Provincial Championship\">{{Cite web | url=https://www.provincial.rugby/npc/about | title=About the National Provincial Championship | website=provincial.rugby | year=2026 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref>\n\nAll provincial teams play ten games during the regular season (round robin): five games are played at the provincial union's home ground and five games are away games. The seven teams that finished the previous season in 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th, 9th, 11th and 13th place will play each other as well as four cross-over matches against the teams that finished 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th and 14th. Likewise, the teams that finished 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th and 14th will also play each other as well as four cross-over games against the provinces that were ranked 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th, 9th, 11th or 13th at the end of the previous season.<ref name=\"Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026\" /><ref name=\"About the National Provincial Championship\"/> The ten round robin games in 2026 are played over a period of ten weeks (starting on 30 July 2026).<ref name=\"NZR releases an exciting schedule marking 50 years of NPC\">{{Cite press release | url=https://www.nzrugby.co.nz/news-and-events/latest-news/new-zealand-rugby-releases-an-exciting-schedule-marking-50-years-of-npc | title=NZR releases an exciting schedule marking 50 years of NPC | work=New Zealand Rugby | date=25 March 2026 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref><ref name=\"National Provincial Championship Fixtures & Results\">{{Cite web | url=https://www.provincial.rugby/npc/fixtures-and-results | title=National Provincial Championship Fixtures & Results | website=provincial.rugby | year=2026 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref>\n\nThe regular season is followed by quarter-finals to be played by the eight highest ranked teams on the competition table. The finals rankings are determined by the number of competition points earned during the regular season. Competition points can be gained in the following way: four points are awarded to the winning team, two points to each team for a draw, and no points for a loss. Teams can also receive a [[Rugby union bonus points system|bonus point]] if they score four or more tries in a match, or lose by seven points or less.<ref name=\"Adjustment to Mitre 10 Heartland Championship bonus points system\">{{cite news| title=Adjustment to Mitre 10 Heartland Championship bonus points system | url=http://rugbyheartland.co.nz/wp/2016/09/02/adjustment-to-mitre-10-heartland-championship-bonus-points-system/ | website=RugbyHeartland.co.nz | date=2 September 2016 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref>\n\nIf two or more provincial teams finish with an equal number of competition points, the following [[tiebreaker]] rules apply. If ''two'' unions are tied, the union which has defeated the other in a head-to-head is placed higher. In case of a draw between them, the side with the biggest [[points difference]] is ranked higher. If ''three or more'' unions are tied and they have all played each other, the team with most competition points in that year against the other tied unions is ranked higher; if they have not all played each other, the team with the biggest points difference in the round robin is ranked higher. In all cases, if these unions are still tied, the ranking is decided by the highest number of tries scored, the most points scored, or a [[coin toss]].<ref name=\"Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026\" />\n\nThe quarter-finals are played as follows, with the highest ranked team hosting:\n\n:'''QF 1''': 1 v 8;\n:'''QF 2''': 2 v 7;\n:'''QF 3''': 3 v 6; and\n:'''QF 4''': 4 v 5.\n\nIn the semi-finals, the highest-ranked quarter-final winner hosts the lowest-ranked quarter-final winner and the second highest-ranked quarter-final winner hosts the third highest-ranked quarter-final winner.\n\nThe NPC Final will be played between the two semi-final winners, again at the home venue of the team with the higher finals ranking.<ref name=\"Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026\" /><ref name=\"About the National Provincial Championship\"/>\n\nThe rules governing the [[Ranfurly Shield]] state that every home game during the regular season played by the union that holds the Ranfurly Shield is a mandatory challenge match. No challenge matches will be played after the regular season has ended (i.e., during the finals). A holder who competes in the [[National Provincial Championship (2006–present)|NPC]] must also accept at least two challenges from unions competing in the [[Heartland Championship]], including a challenge lodged by the winner of the [[Meads Cup]] at the end of the previous season. These non-mandatory challenge matches must be played before the start of the NPC season.<ref name=\"Competitions Regulations Handbook 2026\" />\n\n==Standings==\nThe current standings for the 2026 National Provincial Championship are:<!-- This sentence can be removed after the regular season -->\n\n{{#invoke:sports table|main|style=Rugby\n\n<!-- Team names, do not change; exceptions: move (RS) if the Ranfurly Shield holder changes; after the final, add (RU) after the name of the losing finalist -->\n| name_AKL = {{rut|Auckland}}\n| name_BOP = {{rut|Bay of Plenty}}\n| name_CAN = {{rut|Canterbury}}\n| name_COU = {{rut|Counties Manukau}} '''(RS)'''\n| name_HBY = {{rut|Hawke's Bay}}\n| name_MAN = {{rut|Manawatu}}\n| name_NHA = {{rut|North Harbour}}\n| name_NOR = {{rut|Northland}}\n| name_OTA = {{rut|Otago}} \n| name_SOU = {{rut|Southland}}\n| name_TAR = {{rut|Taranaki}}\n| name_TAS = {{rut|Tasman}}\n| name_WAI = {{rut|Waikato}}\n| name_WEL = {{rut|Wellington}}\n\n<!-- Update results here and then (if needed) classification below. Loss\n|No game\n|colspan=\"2\" style=\"background:#ECECEC\"| DNQ = did not qualify\n|colspan=\"14\"|\n|}\n\n==Regular season==\nDuring the regular season of the 2026 National Provincial Championship, each team plays 10 games over a period of ten weeks. The competition started on 30 July 2026, when {{rut|Waikato}} hosted {{Rut|Bay of Plenty}}. The first [[Ranfurly Shield]] defence of the season takes place on 9 August 2026, when {{Rut|Otago}} hosts {{Rut|Canterbury}}.<ref name=\"National Provincial Championship Fixtures & Results\"/><ref winners {{Rut|Mid Canterbury}} were the [[Heartland Championship]] teams to challenge {{Rut|Otago}} for the [[Ranfurly Shield]] in 2026.<ref>{{Cite Instagram |user=otagorugbyteam |postid=DVpdhxPDYCN date=10 March 2026 | access-date=4 June 2026}}</ref>\n\nOtago Rugby confirmed on 18 May 2026 that the game against North Otago would be played on Tuesday 16 June 2026 at {{Rus|Forsyth Barr Stadium}}, season\n}}\nThe '''2026 Super Rugby Pacific season'''{{efn|Known as the '''Swyftx Super Rugby Pacific''' in [[Australia]]<ref>{{cite news |last=Sim |first=Josh |date=10 October 2025 |title=Super Rugby nabs 'multimillion-dollar' Swyftx crypto title deal in Australia |url=https://www.sportspro.com/sponsorship-marketing/sponsorship/super-rugby-pacific-women-swyftx-title-sponsorship-october-2025/ |work=[[SportsPro]]}}</ref> and '''Shop N Save Super Rugby Pacific''' in [[Fiji]] for [[Naming rights|sponsorship reasons]].<ref>{{cite web |title=Shop N Save Supermarket Renews Sponsorship With Fijian Drua For Three More Seasons |url=https://drua.rugby/content/shop-n-save-supermarket-renews-sponsorship-with-fijian-drua-for-three-more-seasons |date=9 January 2026 |website=drua.rugby |publisher=[[Fiji Rugby Union]] |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260109031504/https://drua.rugby/content/shop-n-save-supermarket-renews-sponsorship-with-fijian-drua-for-three-more-seasons |archive-date=9 January 2026}}</ref>}} was the 31st season of [[Super Rugby]], an annual [[rugby union]] competition organised by [[SANZAAR]] between teams from [[Australia]], [[Fiji]], [[New Zealand]] and a combined team from [[Samoa]], [[Tonga]] and other [[List of islands in the Pacific Ocean|Pacific Island]] nations. It was the 30th anniversary of the competition, having been established in [[1996 Super 12 season|1996]].<ref name=draw>{{cite web |title=Super Rugby celebrates 30th anniversary, unveils 2026 season draw |url=https://super.rugby/superrugby/news/super-rugby-celebrates-30th-anniversary-unveils-2026-season-draw1/ |date=28 August 2025 |website=super.rugby |publisher=[[SANZAAR]] |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250828002001/https://super.rugby/superrugby/news/super-rugby-celebrates-30th-anniversary-unveils-2026-season-draw1/ |archive-date=28 August 2025 |access-date=28 August 2025 }}</ref> The fixtures were released on 28 August 2025 and featured the return of [[Super Round]] in [[Christchurch]], [[New Zealand]].<ref>{{cite news |last=Thorpe |first=Mike |date=11 August 2025 |title=Christchurch's new stadium to debut with Super Rugby Pacific Super Round 2026 |url=https://www.nzherald.co.nz/nz/christchurch/christchurchs-new-stadium-to-debut-with-super-rugby-pacific-super-round-2026/K23UDCMY7JDVVHPNABIQ7UZBGI/ |work=The New Zealand Herald |url-access=subscription }}</ref>\n\nThe defending champions were the [[Crusaders (rugby union)|Crusaders]], who won their 13th title in 2025.<ref>{{cite news |date=21 June 2025 |title=Live: Crusaders v Chiefs – Super Rugby Pacific final |url=https://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/360730728/crusaders-v-chiefs-all-you-need-know-about-super-rugby-pacific-final |url-status=live |work=Stuff |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250621085639/https://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/360730728/crusaders-v-chiefs-all-you-need-know-about-super-rugby-pacific-final |archive-date=21 June 2025 }}</ref><ref>{{cite news |date=21 June 2025 |title=Super Rugby Pacific: Crusaders beat Chiefs to win title |url-access=subscription |url=https://www.nzherald.co.nz/sport/rugby/super-rugby/super-rugby-pacific-final-live-updates-crusaders-and-chiefs-clash-for-the-title/6OV43VKBE5FBXLELZL437CYT5M/ |work=The New Zealand Herald }}</ref> The [[Hurricanes (rugby union)|Hurricanes]] won the 2026 Super Rugby Pacific title, defeating the [[Chiefs (Super Rugby)|Chiefs]] emphatically in [[2026 Super Rugby Pacific final|the final]] to win their second Super Rugby championship.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/360995654/hurricanes-pound-chiefs-wellington-win-their-first-super-rugby-title-decade|title=Hurricanes pound the Chiefs in Wellington to win their first Super Rugby title in a decade|first=Aaron|last=Goile|website=[[Stuff (website)|Stuff]]|date=20 June 2026|access-date=28 June 2026}}</ref>\n\n==Format and competition==\nUnchanged from last season ([[2025 Super Rugby Pacific season|2025]]), eleven teams competed in a [[Round-robin tournament|round-robin]] format, playing fourteen matches each (seven home, seven away), with two byes.<ref name=draw/> Teams faced four \"rivals\" twice and six others once. The top six advanced to the [[#Finals series|qualifying finals]]: 1st vs 6th, 2nd vs 5th, and 3rd vs 4th. The winners and the highest-seeded losing team progressed to the semi-finals. Newly introduced for 2026,<ref name=draw/> rather than losing one seeding rank as in the [[2025 Super Rugby Pacific season|2025 season]], the highest-ranked losing team entered as the fourth seed, playing away in the [[semi-finals]].<ref name=draw/><ref>{{cite news |last=Mitchell |first=Brittany |date=27 August 2025 |title=Fan backlash sees Super Rugby Pacific finals 'lucky loser' rules changed |url=https://www.espn.com.au/rugby/story/_/id/46084368/fan-backlash-sees-super-rugby-pacific-finals-lucky-loser-rules-change |publisher=[[ESPN]] |access-date=28 August 2025 }}</ref>\n\nStandings were based on competition points: four for a win, two for a draw, and none for a loss. [[Rugby union bonus points system|Bonus points]] were awarded for scoring three or more tries than the opponent or for losing by seven points or fewer.<ref name=format>{{cite web |url=https://super.rugby/superrugby/about-super-rugby/tournament-format/ |title=Tournament Format – Super Rugby Pacific |year=2024 |work=SANZAAR }}</ref>\n\n===Law changes===\nIn February 2026, just weeks before the start of the season, it was revealed that some of the existing laws pertaining to [[Glossary of rugby union terms#R|restarts]], kicking/territory, [[Rugby union gameplay#Ruck|rucks]], and the [[television match official]] (TMO), would be amended in order to reduce stoppages, keep the ball-in-play longer, and simplify the viewing experience.<ref>{{cite news |date=4 February 2026 |title=Super Rugby Pacific law changes: What's new for the 2026 season's kick-off |url=https://www.nzherald.co.nz/sport/rugby/super-rugby/super-rugby-pacific-law-changes-whats-new-for-2025-season-kick-off/OND7ER7QEBA6TKI5SAEKSYBV7M/ |work=[[The New Zealand Herald]] |publisher=[[New Zealand Media and Entertainment]] |access-date=5 February 2026 |url-access=subscription }}</ref><ref>{{cite news |date=3 February 2026 |title=Why Super Rugby Pacific bosses have told TMOs to stop interfering in matches |url=https://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/360932686/why-super-rugby-pacific-bosses-have-told-tmos-stop-interfering-matches |work=[[Stuff (website)|Stuff]] |publisher=[[Stuff (company)|Stuff Ltd]] |access-date=5 February 2026 }}</ref><ref>{{cite magazine |date=2 February 2026 |title=Super Rugby reins in TMO, trials five laws |url=https://www.sarugbymag.co.za/super-rugby-reins-in-tmo-trials-five-laws/ |magazine=[[SA Rugby Magazine]] |access-date=5 February 2026 }}</ref> The law changes, as laid out by ''[[The Sydney Morning Herald]]'', were:<ref name=smh-law>{{cite news |last=Drennan |first=Jonathan |date=4 February 2026 |title='Most ridiculous decision': Waugh slams Super Rugby criticism from referee behind infamous Bledisloe call |url=https://www.smh.com.au/sport/rugby-union/most-ridiculous-decision-waugh-slams-super-rugby-criticism-from-referee-behind-infamous-bledisloe-call-20260203-p5nz9n.html |work=[[The Sydney Morning Herald]] |publisher=[[Nine Entertainment]] |access-date=5 February 2026 }}</ref>\n\n* It will no longer be mandatory for the referee to issue a [[Penalty card#Rugby union|yellow]] or [[Penalty card#Red card|red card]] to a player on the defending team when awarding a [[Try (rugby)#Penalty try|penalty try]]. Any sanction will be at the discretion of the referee. (Law 8.3)\n* Accidental [[Offside (rugby)|offsides]] and teams delaying playing the ball away from a [[Rugby union gameplay#Ruck|ruck]] will result in [[Free kick (rugby union)|free kicks]] rather than [[Scrum (rugby union)|scrums]]. (Law 10.5 and Law 15.17)\n* After the referee has called \"use it\" at the ruck, no additional players from the team in possession may join the ruck. (Law 15.17)\n* Teams will be permitted to pass the ball back into their half before kicking a [[Glossary of rugby union terms#50|50:22]]. (Law 18.8a)\n* Players will be allowed to take [[Glossary of rugby union terms#T|quick taps]] within one metre either side of the mark, or anywhere behind the mark, if they are within that two-metre channel running parallel to the touchlines. (Law 20.2)\n* The [[television match official]] (TMO) will only intervene unprompted if the referee has overlooked an act of serious foul play (yellow card level or above), or a clear and obvious infringement leading to a try.\n\nThese changes were reported to have \"emphatic support\" amongst Super Rugby clubs, match officials and stakeholders.<ref>{{cite web |title=Super Rugby Pacific confirms new law innovations for 2026 season |url=https://www.rugby.com.au/news/super-rugby-pacific-confirms-new-law-innovations-for-2026-season-202621 |date=2 February 2026 |website=[[Rugby.com.au]] |access-date=5 February 2026 }}</ref> Sports news website ''Planet Rugby'' gave a positive assessment about law changes that give referees more discretion and help speed the game up, particularly removing the mandatory card for penalty tries, tightening the \"use it\" ruck law to kill caterpillars, and allowing more flexibility for quick taps.<ref name=planet-laws>{{cite web |title=Super Rugby law trials judged as the south risks taking sport down 'slippery slope' despite ending the 'bane' of the game |url=https://www.planetrugby.com/news/super-rugby-law-trials-judged-as-the-south-risks-taking-sport-down-slippery-slope-despite-ending-the-bane-of-rugby |last=Newboult |first=Colin |date=3 February 2026 |website=Planet Rugby |access-date=5 February 2026 }}</ref>\n\n==Teams and personnel==\n{{Further|Super Rugby franchise areas}}\n\n===Overview===\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n! rowspan=2| Union\n! rowspan=2| Team\n! colspan=2| Stadia information\n! rowspan=2| Coach\n! rowspan=2| Captain\n|-\n! points for a win <br/>* 2 competition points for a draw <br/>* 1 competition bonus point for a loss by seven points or less <br/>* 1 competition bonus point for scoring at least three tries more than the opponent in a match\n|-\n| '''Tiebreaker rules:''' <br/>If, at any stage, teams have the same number of competition points, the following [[tiebreaker]] rules apply (in this order) to determine their standing: <br/>* Most wins from all matches<br/>* Highest difference between total points for and total points against from all matches ([[points difference]])<br/>* Most tries scored from all matches<br/>* Highest difference between total tries for and total tries against from all matches<br/>* [[Coin toss]]\n|}\n\n===Round-by-round===\nThe table below shows each team's progression [[Newcastle Jets FC|Newcastle Jets]] are the defending premiers while Auckland FC are defending champions.\n\n== Clubs ==\n\n===Stadiums and locations===\nTwelve clubs are participating in the 2026–27 season|2027]]''\n}}\n\nThe '''2026 NRL season''' was the 119th season of professional [[rugby league]] in Australia and the 29th season run by the [[National Rugby League]] (NRL). The regular season was followed by a finals series contested by the top eight teams on the competition ladder. The [[Penrith Panthers]] were the minor premiers, while the [[Sydney Roosters]] defeated the [[Newcastle Knights]] in the Grand Final to win their 16th premiership.\n\nThe season recorded the highest aggregate and average regular season crowds in first-grade rugby league history, surpassing the record set the previous year.\n\nThe season was also the highest scoring in first-grade rugby league history, with 10,167 points scored across 213 games, surpassing the 253 game [[1998 NRL season|1998 season]] in which 10,131 points were scored. At an average of 47.73 points scored per match, 2026 was also the highest average scoring season since [[2004 NRL season|2004]].{{efn|in which 9,086 points were scored across 189 matches, an average of 48.07 points per game.}}\n\n==Teams==\nThe lineup of teams in the league remained unchanged for the 4th and final consecutive year, with seventeen participating in the regular season: ten from [[New South Wales]], four from [[Queensland]], and one from each of [[Victoria (state)|Victoria]], the [[Australian Capital Territory]], and [[New Zealand]].\n\n{| class=\"wikitable\" 57,102\n|}\n===Bracket===\n{{transcluded section|2026 NRL finals series}}\n{{#section-h:2026 NRL finals series|Bracket}}\n\n==Player statistics and records==\n*In Round 2, [[Alex Johnston (rugby league)|Alex Johnston]] surpassed [[Ken Irvine]]'s record of 212 tries in the NRL and its predecessor competitions.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.news.com.au/sport/nrl/rabbitohs-vs-roosters-live-nrl-makes-call-on-fans-storming-the-field-for-recordbreaker/news-story/9d6328382ea3ea3b89794e6674b148fd|title=Rabbitohs vs Roosters: Alex Johnston breaks tryscoring record, fans storm field in wild scenes|date=14 March 2026|access-date=15 April 2026|website=[[News.com.au]]|first=Matthew|last=Sullivan}}</ref>\n*In Round 5, [[Thomas Jenkins (rugby league)|Thomas Jenkins]] became the first player to score 12 tries in their first 5 games of a season, having tied the record of 10 tries in the first 4 games the previous week.\n*In Round 7, [[Thomas Jenkins (rugby league)|Thomas Jenkins]] became the first player in the NRL era to score 2 or more tries in 7 consecutive games.\n*In Round 9, [[Alex Johnston (rugby league)|Alex Johnston]] became the first player in the NRL and its predecessor competitions to score 17 career hat-tricks. Johnson also became the first player to score 100 or more tries at a single venue, that being [[Stadium Australia|Accor Stadium]].\n*In Round 10, [[Jason Taumalolo]] surpassed former teammate [[Johnathan Thurston]] as the most capped [[North Queensland Cowboys]] player of all time.\n*In Round 12, [[Moeaki Fotuaika]] surpassed [[Mark Minichiello]] as the most capped [[Gold Coast Titans]] player of all time.\n*In Round 12, [[Alex Johnston (rugby league)|Alex Johnston]] became the first player in the NRL era to score a try in each of their first ten games of a season, surpassing [[Kayal Iro|KL Iro]] who scored in his first nine games in 2025.\n*In Round 19, [[Daniel Tupou]] became the fifth player to play 300 games for the [[Sydney Roosters]].\n*In Round 24, [[Jamayne Isaako]] became the fastest player to score 1,000 points for a single club, doing so in his 93rd game for the [[Dolphins (NRL)|Dolphins]].{{efn|This figure is not adjusted by era; previous record holder [[Mick Cronin (rugby league)|Mick Cronin]] accomplished the feat in 98 games when a try was only worth 3 points.}}\n*In Round 25, [[Phillip Sami]] surpassed former teammate [[Anthony Don]] as the greatest tryscorer in [[Gold Coast Titans]] history.\n\nThe following statistics are as of the conclusion of the finals series.\n{{col-begin}}\n{{col-2}}\n'''Top 5 point scorers'''\n{| class=\"wikitable\" dates|date=October 2022}}\n{{Infobox rugby league international tournament\n| year               = 2026\n| title              = Men's Rugby League World Cup\n| image              = \n| imagesize          = \n| Website            = \n| finalists          = 10\n| winners            = \n| count              = \n| matches            = 18\n| attendance         = \n| points             = \n| tries              = \n| topscorer-flag     = Samoa\n| topscorer          = [[Stephen Crichton]] (26)\n| top try scorer-flag= Samoa\n| top try scorer     = [[Sualauvi Fa'alogo]] (4)\n| tournaments        = Rugby League World Cup\n| last               = [[2021 Men's Rugby League World Cup|2021]]\n| next               = [[2030 Men's Rugby League World Cup|2030]]\n|country=Australia|country2=Papua New Guinea|country3=New Zealand}}\n\nThe '''2026 Men's Rugby League World Cup''' is the seventeenth staging of the [[Rugby League World Cup]], and is one of three major tournaments which make up the [[2026 Rugby League World Cup]].\n\nThe competition was [[2025 Rugby League World Cup|to be held in October and  November 2025]],<ref>{{cite web |title=2025 Rugby League World Cup: France promises low carbon event after being named host |url=https://www.sportspromedia.com/news/rugby-league-world-cup-2025-france-host-sustainability/ |website=SportsPro |access-date=25 March 2022 |date=11 January 2022}}</ref> but was moved to 2026 following the withdrawal of France as the host nation.<ref name=\"26Confirmed1\">{{Cite web|url=https://www.bbc.com/sport/rugby-league/66390816|title=Southern hemisphere to host 2026 World Cup|date=3 August 2023|website=BBC Sport|accessdate=6 August 2023}}</ref><ref name=\"26Confirmed2\">{{Cite web|url=https://www.theguardian.com/sport/2023/aug/03/rugby-league-world-cup-announced-2026|title=Reduced Rugby League World Cup to take place in 2026 |website=The Guardian|date=3 August 2023 |accessdate=6 August 2023}}</ref> The competition will run in parallel with the women's and wheelchair tournaments.<ref>{{cite news |title=France 2025: 13 questions answered about the Rugby League World Cup |url=https://www.theguardian.com/sport/no-helmets-required/2022/jan/19/france-2025-rugby-league-world-cup-england |access-date=25 March 2022 |work=[[The Guardian]] |date=19 January 2022}}</ref><ref name=\"comps1\">{{Cite web|date=11 January 2022|title='The Rooster is going to sing again': France aiming high for 2025 World Cup|url=https://www.nrl.com/news/2022/01/11/the-rooster-is-going-to-sing-again-france-aiming-high-for-2025-world-cup/|access-date=13 January 2022|website=National Rugby League|language=en}}</ref><ref name=\"EverythingYouNeedToKnow\">{{cite web |date=11 January 2022 |title=Everything you need to know about the 2025 World Cup in France |url=https://www.loverugbyleague.com/post/everything-you-need-to-now-france-rugby-league-world-cup/}}</ref>\n\n==Format==\nDue to the rescheduling, the competition features 10 teams, similar to the [[1995 Rugby League World Cup|1995]] and [[2008 Rugby League World Cup|2008]] editions, reduced from 16.<ref name=\"26Confirmed1\" /><ref name=\"26Confirmed2\" /> This was done partly to aid in the late rescheduling of the tournament, but also to boost commercial appeal of the tournament after criticism of blowout scorelines in the early stages of the [[2021 Men's Rugby League World Cup|2021 World Cup]].<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.theguardian.com/sport/no-helmets-required/2023/aug/04/rugby-league-swaps-inclusion-pragmatism-next-world-cup-irl|title=Rugby league swaps inclusion for pragmatism at next World Cup|first=Gavin|last=Willacy|date=4 August 2023|accessdate=23 November 2025|website=The Guardian}}</ref>\n\n==Host selection==\n{{see also|2025 Rugby League World Cup#Host selection}}\nThe [[International Rugby League]] (IRL) originally decided in 2016 to have the tournaments hosted in the United States and Canada.<ref name=\"America1\">{{cite news \n  | title = Rugby League World Cup moves to North America in 2025\n  | publisher = stuff sport\n  | date = 20 November 2016\n| url = http://www.stuff.co.nz/sport/league/86672646/rugby-league-world-cup-moves-to-north-america-in-2025\n  | accessdate = 21 November 2016}}</ref><ref name=\"America2\">{{Cite web |date=2016-12-01 |title=North America to Host 2025 Rugby league World Cup |url=https://www.canadarugbyleague.com/north-america-host-2025-rugby-league-world-cup/ |access-date=2023-05-17 |website=Canada Rugby League Association |language=en-CA}}</ref> In December 2019 however, the IRL withdrew the hosting rights due to the promoters, Moore Sports International, being unable to guarantee the staging of the tournaments.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://www.smh.com.au/sport/nrl/us-world-cup-hosting-plans-torpedoed-by-money-trouble-20181204-p50k5p.html |title=US World Cup hosting plans torpedoed by money trouble |newspaper=Sydney Morning Herald |author= Adrian Proszenko |date=4 December 2018 |access-date= 25 March 2022}}</ref><ref name=\"torpedoed\">{{cite news|url=https://www.smh.com.au/sport/nrl/us-world-cup-hosting-plans-torpedoed-by-money-trouble-20181204-p50k5p.html |title=US World Cup hosting plans torpedoed by money trouble |newspaper=Sydney Morning Herald |author= Adrian Proszenko |date=4 December 2018 |access-date= 28 December 2018}}</ref>\n\nAfter re-opening the bidding to host the tournaments, the IRL awarded the hosting rights to France.<ref name=\"France1\">{{Cite web|date=8 January 2022|title=France to host 2025 World Cup|url=https://www.nrl.com/news/2022/01/09/france-to-host-2025-world-cup/|access-date=8 January 2022|website=National Rugby League}}</ref><ref name=\"France3\">{{Cite web |last= |first= |date=2022-01-11 |title=France set to host the 2025 Rugby League World Cups |url=https://asiapacificrl.com/2022/01/11/france-set-to-host-the-2025-rugby-league-world-cups/ |access-date=2023-05-17 |website=Asia Pacific Rugby League |language=en-US |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220127060604/https://asiapacificrl.com/2022/01/11/france-set-to-host-the-2025-rugby-league-world-cups/ |archive-date=2022-01-27}}</ref> On 15 May 2023, the France 2025 organising committee was forced to withdraw from hosting the tournament, due to financial concerns from the [[Borne government|new French government]] elected in May 2022.<ref name=\"Withdraw1\">{{cite news |title=France pulls out of hosting Rugby League World Cup |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-league/65601619 |access-date=15 May 2023 |work=BBC Sport |date=15 May 2023}}</ref><ref name=\"Withdraw3\">{{cite news |last1=Bower |first1=Aaron |title=2025 Rugby League World Cup in doubt after France pull out of staging event |url=https://www.theguardian.com/sport/2023/may/15/2025-rugby-league-world-cup-in-doubt-after-france-pull-out-of-staging-event |access-date=15 May 2023 |work=[[The Guardian]] |date=15 May 2023}}</ref>\n\nOn 3 August 2023 it was confirmed that the tournament would be moved to 2026 and held in the Southern Hemisphere.<ref name=\"26Confirmed1\" /><ref name=\"26Confirmed2\" /> On 24 July 2024, the IRL announced \nthat [[Australia]] would host the 2026 tournament, with a number of games played in [[Papua New Guinea]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-07-24 |title=Australia to host 2026 World Cup |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-league/articles/czd92pqdj81o |access-date=2024-07-24 |website=BBC Sport |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.nrl.com/news/2024/07/24/arlc-welcomes-rugby-league-world-cup-2026-hosting-rights/|title=ARLC welcomes Rugby League World Cup 2026 hosting rights|date=24 July 2024|website=National Rugby League}}</ref>\n\n== Teams ==\n=== Qualification ===\n{{main|2026 Men's Rugby League World Cup qualification}}\n\nOn 3 August 2023, the date the tournament was announced, the eight quarter-finalists of the [[2021 Men's Rugby League World Cup|2021 World Cup]] (who were all previously qualified for the cancelled 2025 World Cup) were confirmed to have qualified for the tournament.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Ashes series, Kangaroo, Kiwi tours revived in new International Rugby League Calendar |url=https://www.intrl.sport/news/ashes-series-kangaroo-kiwi-tours-revived-in-international-calendar/ |access-date=2023-08-10 |website=Rugby League International Federation}}</ref> The qualification process for the remaining two slots was announced on 7 October 2023, and ran from 2024 to 2025.<ref name=\"qualification\">{{Cite web |title=International Rugby League Board confirms details for World Series, World Cups |url=https://www.intrl.sport/news/irl-board-update-details-confirmed-for-world-series-world-cups/ |access-date=2023-10-08 |website=Rugby League International Federation}}</ref>\n{{#lst:2026 Men's Rugby League World Cup qualification|Teams}}\n\n===Draw===\n{{rlnt|AUS}} and {{rlnt|NZL}} played in the opening game at the [[Sydney Football Stadium (2022)|Sydney Football Stadium]] on 15 October.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2025-10-22 |title=Rugby League World Cup arrives on Seven and 7plus Sport |url=https://7news.com.au/sport/rugby-league/rugby-league-world-cup-2026-arrives-live-free-and-exclusive-on-seven-and-7plus-sport-c-20432340 |access-date=2025-10-22 |website=7NEWS |language=en}}</ref> {{rlnt|SAM}} and {{rlnt|TON}} will also face each other in the group stage to replicate their successful [[2025 Rugby League Pacific Championships#Men's Pacific Cup|2025 Pacific Championship]] match, to be played at the [[Western Sydney Stadium]].<ref name=\"format\" /> The draw was announced on 23 November 2025.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-league/articles/cr5z48v62d6o|title=Rugby League World Cup 2026: England avoid Australia & NZ in men's draw|date=23 November 2025|website=BBC Sport|accessdate=23 November 2025}}</ref>\n\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!width=25%|Pool A\n!width=25%|Pool B\n!width=25%|Pool section|date=October 2026}}\n\n===On-field rules and disciplinary measures===\n{{Empty section|date=October 2026}}\n\n== Pool stage ==\nIn October 2025, it was reported that the tournament would be played with one group of four and two groups of three, with the top two from Group A advancing to the semi-finals, and the top two teams advancing from the combined Groups B and C. Group A teams will play each other once each, while each member of Group B plays each member of Group C, and vice versa.<ref name=\"format\">{{Cite web|url=https://aapnews.aap.com.au/news/tonga-samoa-set-for-showdown-in-new-world-cup-format/NgdGqIYB|title=Tonga, Samoa set for showdown in new World Cup format|date=29 October 2025|website=AAP News|accessdate=23 November 2025}}</ref>\n\n===Pool A===\n{{Main|2026 Men's Rugby League World Cup Pool A}}\n\n<section begin=WC_GAtable/>{{#invoke:sports (IRL)]|team2=Second semi-final winner|points2=|try2=|goal2=|drop2=|field2=|sinbin2=|sentoff2= |stadium=[[Lang Park]], [[Brisbane]]|attendance=|referee=|referee2=|vreferee=|touch=|manofmatch=|potmaward=|potmwinner=}}<section end=\"WC_F\" />\n\n==Controversy==\n{{see also|2025 Rugby League World Cup#Controversy surrounding the withdrawal}}\nThe reduction of teams for the 2026 World Cup from 16 to 10 gained criticism from players and associations of lower ranked nations for whom it would now be much harder to qualify for the competition and claims that this would stagger growth of rugby league in these countries.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2023-08-09 |title='I'm hugely disappointed' - Ireland captain George King questions downsized Rugby League World Cup format |url=https://www.loverugbyleague.com/post/im-hugely-disappointed-ireland-captain-george-king-questions-downsized-rugby-league-world-cup-format |access-date=2023-08-10 |website=LoveRugbyLeague |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Cook |first=Sam |date=2023-08-05 |title=Brazil Rugby League launch scathing attack on IRL and say World Cup is only for 'Englishmen, Australians and their descendants' |url=https://www.seriousaboutrl.com/brazil-rugby-league-launch-scathing-attack-on-irl-and-say-world-cup-is-only-for-englishmen-australians-and-their-descendants-79732/ |access-date=2023-08-10 |website=Rugby League News |language=en-GB}}</ref> On 22 August 2023, it was reported that a letter signed by 16 member associations was sent to [[International Rugby League]] protesting against the reduction of teams and ban on affiliate members.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.loverugbyleague.com/post/exclusive-16-countries-hit-out-at-reduced-2026-world-cup-format-with-official-letter-sent-to-international-rugby-league|title=Exclusive: 16 countries hit out at reduced 2026 World Cup format with official letter sent to international rugby league|date=22 August 2023|website=Love Rugby League}}</ref>\n\n[[International Rugby League]] (IRL) claimed the reduction was to increase the competitiveness of games thus showcasing the sport better. The IRL claimed having lower ranked nations at the 2021 tournament came at a \"significant cost\" and did not bring \"commercial benefits\".<ref>{{Cite news |last=Willacy |first=Gavin |date=2023-08-04 |title=Rugby league swaps inclusion for pragmatism at next World Cup |language=en-GB |work=The Guardian |url=https://www.theguardian.com/sport/no-helmets-required/2023/aug/04/rugby-league-swaps-inclusion-pragmatism-next-world-cup-irl |access-date=2023-08-10 |issn=0261-3077}}</ref>\n\nA ban on affiliate members was not put in place for the [[2026 Women's Rugby League World Cup|women's]] and [[2026 Wheelchair Rugby League World Cup|wheelchair]] world cups.<ref name=\"qualification\" />\n\nAhead of the qualification process, {{rlnt|WAL}} then-head coach [[John Kear]] criticised the reduction, stating it sent the wrong message about IRL's commitment to grow the international game and skews qualification in favour of Oceanian teams.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-10-16 |title=Wales boss John Kear hits out at World Cup qualification |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-league/articles/c4g433leyj9o |access-date=2025-10-23 |website=BBC Sport |language=en-GB}}</ref>\n\n==See also==\n{{Portal|Sports}}\n* [[2026 Women's Rugby League World Cup]]\n* [[2026 Wheelchair Rugby League World Cup]]\n\n==Notes==\n{{notelist}}\n\n==References==\n{{reflist}}\n\n{{RLWC2026}}\n{{Rlwc}}\n{{2026 RLWC venues}}\n{{2026 in rugby 2025–26 Big Bash League season\n| next_year = 2027–28\n| next_tournament = Big Bash League\n}}\n\nThe '''2026–27 Big Bash League season''' or '''BBL|16''' will be the sixteenth edition of the [[Big Bash League]] (BBL). The tournament will be  played from 12 December 2026, with the final held on 26 January 2027. The [[Perth Scorchers]] are the defending champions.\n\nThe opening match, set to be held at the [[M. A. Chidambaram Stadium]] in [[Chennai]], will mark the first time that a match will be played in a venue outside of Australia.\n\n== Background ==\nThe [[Big Bash League]] is a professional men's [[Twenty20 cricket]] tournament played in Australia and organised by [[Cricket Australia]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=14 July 2026 |title=Christmas Eve cricket returns as full BBL{{!}}16 schedule released |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4534480/bbl16-big-bash-league-season-schedule-fixture-announced-dates-chennai-christmas-eve-canberra-melbourne-sydney-perth-launceston |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=14 July 2026}}</ref> It was first held in [[2011–12 Big Bash League season|2011–12]], with the 2026–27 season marking its sixteenth edition. The fifteenth edition was won by the [[Perth Scorchers]], who defeated the [[Sydney Sixers]] in the final.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Lavalette |first=Tristan |date=25 January 2026 |url=https://www.cricinfo.com/series/big-bash-league-2025-26-1490534/perth-scorchers-vs-sydney-sixers-final-1493281/match-report |title=Payne and Richardson set up Scorchers to claim sixth BBL title|work=Cricinfo |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Skelton |first=Jack |date=25 January 2026 |title=Scorchers crush Sixers to win sixth Big Bash title |url=https://www.bbc.com/sport/cricket/articles/crle1nz63r0o |website=BBC Sport |access-date=25 January 2026}}</ref>\n\n=== Format changes ===\n\"Designated batters\" will be implemented for the first time.<ref>{{cite web |last=Nice |first=Christ |date=15 January 2026 |title=BBL to introduce 'designated batters' from next season |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4434311/big-bash-to-introduce-designated-batter-fielder-new-rule-for-bbl16-next-season-details-explained |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> Similar to the \"X-Factor player\" from [[2020–21 Big Bash League season|2020–21]], teams will be able to nominate a player who will not be allowed to field or bowl. Over-rate penalty rules will also be changed, enforcing full [[Powerplay (cricket)|powerplay]] restrictions on teams who breach the over-rate timer.<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=31 August 2026 |title=Big Bash batters to benefit from change to over-rate penalties |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4568917/big-bash-league-batters-to-benefit-from-tougher-over-rate-penalties-bbl16-wbbl12-playing-conditions-changes-countdown-timer-powerplay |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref>\n\n== Teams ==\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|+\n!scope=\"col\"| Team\n!scope=\"col\"| Home ground(s)\n!scope=\"col\"| Captain\n!scope=\"col\"| Head coach<ref name=\"SquadList\" />\n|-\n| [[2026–27 Adelaide Strikers season|Adelaide Strikers]]\n| [[Adelaide Oval]]\n| [[Matt Short]]\n| [[Tim Paine]]\n|-\n| [[2026–27 Brisbane Heat season|Brisbane Heat]]\n| [[The Gabba]]\n| \n| [[James Hopes]]\n|-\n|rowspan=\"2\"| [[2026–27 Hobart Hurricanes season|Hobart Hurricanes]]\n| [[Bellerive Oval]]\n|rowspan=\"2\"| [[Nathan Ellis]]\n|rowspan=\"2\"| [[Jeff Vaughan]]\n|-\n| [[York Park]]\n|-\n|rowspan=\"3\"| [[2026–27 Melbourne Renegades season|Melbourne Renegades]]\n| [[Melbourne Cricket Ground]]\n|rowspan=\"3\"| [[Will Sutherland]]<ref>{{cite press release |author=<!-- not stated --> |date=16 July 2026 |title=Skipper Sutherland embracing Renegades' new chapter |url=https://www.melbournerenegades.com.au/news/4536635/fixtures |publisher=Melbourne Renegades |access-date=3 September 2026}}</ref>\n|rowspan=\"3\"| [[Luke Ronchi]]\n|-\n| [[M. Coaching changes ====\nFollowing [[Greg Shipperd]]'s departure from the [[Sydney Sixers]] and [[Cricket NSW]] in January 2026,<ref>{{cite web |last=Schonafinger |first=Josh |date=31 January 2026 |title=Legendary coach Shipperd to part ways with NSW, Sixers |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4441746/greg-shipperd-new-south-wales-coach-sydney-sixers-big-bash-mentor-master-released-parted-ways |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> [[Matthew Mott]] was named as the coach of the team in July.<ref>{{cite press release |date=22 July 2026 |title=Sydney Sixers appoint Matthew Mott as BBL Head Coach |url=https://www.sydneysixers.com.au/news/4539667/sydney-sixers-appoint-matthew-mott-as-bbl-head-coach |website=Sydney Sixers |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> Previously, [[James Hopes]] had been named head coach, but had stepped away from the position in June to work with the [[Brisbane Heat]] following the resignation of [[Johan Botha (cricketer)|Johan Botha]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Cameron |first=Louis |date=22 June 2026 |title=Hopes quits Sixers after being handed Heat, Bulls jobs |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4527024/james-hopes-appointed-brisbane-heat-queensland-bulls-coach-sydney-sixers-job-shipperd |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> Earlier that month, [[Andrew Flintoff]] was announced to have signed to the [[Sydney Thunder]] as head coach, replacing [[Trevor Bayliss]].<ref>{{cite press release |date=1 June 2026 |title=Flintoff appointed Thunder BBL Head Coach |url=https://www.sydneythunder.com.au/news/4512181 |publisher=Sydney Thunder |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref>\n\nFollowing an internal restructuring at [[Cricket Victoria]], [[Cameron White]] switched from the [[Melbourne Renegades]] to the [[Melbourne Stars]] in July.<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date= |title=White completes crosstown defection amid Stars reshuffle |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4537484/cameron-white-confirmed-as-melbourne-stars-head-coach-peter-moores-director-of-coaching-bbl16-big-bash-league-cricket-victoria |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=3 September 2026}}</ref> [[Luke Ronchi]] was moved from the [[Melbourne Renegades (WBBL)|women's team]] to the men's team.<ref>{{cite press release |date=17 July 2026 |title=Melbourne Renegades appoint Luke Ronchi as Head Coach for BBL{{!}}16 |url=https://www.melbournerenegades.com.au/news/4536701/melbourne-renegades-appoint-luke-ronchi-as-head-coach-for-bbl16 |website=Melbourne Renegades |access-date=3 September 2026}}</ref>\n\n==== Captaincy changes ====\n[[Moises Henriques]] retired from the Big Bash League in July 2026, stepping down as the captain of the [[Sydney Sixers]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=16 July 2026 |title=Henriques calls time on Big Bash, NSW career after 22 years |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4536618/moises-henriques-announces-nsw-sydney-sixers-retirement-most-capped-player-in-australia-domestic-cricket |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref>\n\n== Squads ==\n{{main|List of 2026–27 Big Bash League personnel signings}}\nEach team is allowed to sign an 18-player squad, including a maximum of 12 players signed before 27 August 2026.<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=26 August 2026 |title=Contracting changes set to boost earning power of Big Bash stars |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4566624/big-bash-contracting-changes-announced-boost-earning-power-of-homegrown-australia-stars-overseas-player-draft-wbbl12-bbl16 |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> Teams are allowed to sign additional replacement players in the instance that one of their players is unavailable.<ref>{{cite web |last=Burnett |first=Adam |date=20 May 2014 |title=Dummies Guide to BBL contracts |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/3260980/dummies-guide-to-bbl-contracts |website=Cricket Australia |access-date=3 September 2026}}</ref> stars set for BBL payday under new contracting rules |url=https://www.cricinfo.com/story/australia-and-overseas-stars-set-for-bbl-payday-under-new-contracting-rules-1551458 |website=Cricinfo ==\nEleven venues were selected to host the tournament. [[M. A. Chidambaram Stadium]] in Chennai will become the first venue outside Australia to host a Big Bash League match.<ref>{{Cite web |last=McGlashan |first=Andrew |date=10 July 2026 |url=https://www.cricinfo.com/story/chennai-to-host-bbl-opener-melbourne-renegades-vs-perth-scorchers-1544956 |title=Chennai locked in to host Renegades vs Scorchers BBL opener |access-date=10 July 2026 |work=Cricinfo}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=10 July 2026 |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4532676/melbourne-renegades-perth-scorchers-to-launch-bbl16-season-opener-in-chennai-india-big-bash-league-dates-times?tags=9037 |title=BBL goes to India! Gades to face reigning champs in Chennai |access-date=10 July 2026 |work=Cricket Australia}}</ref> Both [[York Park]] and [[Junction Oval]] will return as venues for the first time since [[2023–24 Big Bash League season|2023]] and [[2022–23 Big Bash League season|2022]], respectively—York Park will host the [[Hobart Hurricanes]], and Junction Oval will host both the [[Melbourne Stars]] and the [[Melbourne Renegades]].<ref>{{cite news |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=8 July 2026 |title=Hurricanes locked in for Launceston return in BBL{{!}}16 |url=https://www.cricket.com.au/news/4532278/hobart-hurricanes-locked-in-for-launceston-return-in-bbl16-big-bash-schedule-update-utas-stadium |work=Cricket Australia |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref><ref name=\"NYEMatch\">{{cite web |date=13 July 2026 |title=Junction Oval to host historic Big Bash League clash on Christmas Eve |url=https://7news.com.au/sport/cricket/junction-oval-to-host-historic-big-bash-league-clash-on-christmas-eve-c-22572145 |website=7 News |access-date=1 September 2026}}</ref> The [[Melbourne Cricket Ground]] will become the Renegades 'primary' home ground, with the team leaving [[Kardinia Park (stadium)|Kardinia Park]] and [[Docklands Stadium]].<ref>{{cite web |last=Paynter |first=Jack |date=10 ''{{ruicon|FRA}} [[Maxime Lucu]] (37)''\n| top try scorer  = ''{{ruicon|NZL}} [[Will Jordan (rugby union)|Will Jordan]] (6)''\n| website    = nationschampionshiprugby.com\n| prevseason =\n| nextseason = 2028\n| teams      = 12\n}}\nThe '''2026 Nations Championship''' is the inaugural edition of the [[Nations Championship]], an international rugby union competition featuring twelve men's teams. The teams are divided into two pools of six, and play a full round-robin of matches with the teams in the opposing pool across the mid year and end-of-year international windows, with the top teams in each pool advancing to a Nations Championship final in London.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Nations Championship is here! |url=https://nationschampionshiprugby.com/ |access-date=17 November 2025 |website=Nations Championship Rugby}}</ref>\n\nOn 17 November 2025, World Rugby confirmed a second competition that would take place concurrently called the [[World Rugby Nations Cup (2026–)|World Rugby Nations Cup]].<ref name=\"WR-1\">{{cite web |title=World Rugby Nations Cup to launch in July 2026 to transform international calendar |url=https://www.world.rugby/news/1018591/world-rugby-nations-cup-to-launch-in-july-2026-to-transform-international-calendar |website=World Rugby |access-date=17 November 2025 |date=17 November 2025}}</ref>\n\nAlthough [[promotion and relegation]] are intended for a future date, there will be no promotion or relegation in the 2026 edition between the Nations Championship and the Nations Cup.\n\n==Participants==\nThe following 12 nations are competing in the 2026 Nations Championship.<ref>{{cite web |title=Nations Championship 2026 |url=https://www.world.rugby/beta/en/tournaments/nations-championship/2026 |website=World Rugby |access-date=15 April 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Nations Championship 2026 & Finals Weekend |url=https://allianzstadiumtwickenham.com/nations-championship |website=Allianz Stadium Twickenham |access-date=15 April 2026}}</ref>\n\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n|-\n!rowspan=\"2\"| Team\n!colspan=\"3\"| Stadium\n!rowspan=\"2\"| Head coach\n!rowspan=\"2\"| Captain\n|-\n! Lake]]\n|}\n'''Notes'''\n{{reflist|group=N}}\n\n==Format==\nThe Nations Championship consists of two geographic pools or conferences; the first, the European conference, consisting of the [[Six Nations Championship|Six Nations]]; the second, the 'Rest of the World' conference, consisting of the [[The Rugby Championship|Rugby Championship]] teams, plus [[Fiji national rugby union team|Fiji]] and [[Japan national rugby union team|Japan]]. Over two international windows each team plays all the teams in the opposite pool once, with three games at home and three away.<ref>{{Cite web |first1=Alan |last1=Dymock |date=24 October 2023 |title=World Rugby confirm Nations Championship details after vote |url=https://www.rugbyworld.com/news/world-rugby-confirm-nations-championship-details-after-vote-162016 |access-date=7 September 2025 |website=Rugby World}}</ref> A cumulative north v south trophy will also be contested in this edition.<ref name=\"BBC\">{{Cite web |last=Henson |first=Mike |date=17 November 2025 |title=Nations Championship to feature north v south hemisphere contest |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-union/articles/c0rpl9v1ly5o |access-date=17 November 2025 |website=BBC Sport}}</ref>\n\nThe standard World Rugby points system applies; teams earn:\n* 4 points for a win\n* 2 points for a draw\n* 0 points for a loss\n* 1 bonus point for scoring 4 or more tries\n* 1 bonus point for losing by fewer than 8 points.\n\n===Fiji home games===\nFiji were required to reverse their home fixtures as their national stadium, the [[HFC Bank Stadium]], does not meet competition requirements. Stadiums of the tournament are required to be 25,000+ seats which the HFC Bank Stadium is just under 10,000 short; in addition, 50% of these must be undercover. Travel logistics and commercial value was also considered for reversing the fixtures.<ref>https://www.fijivillage.com/news/Fiji-does-not-meet-the-requirement-of-hosting-Nations-Championship-games-fx84r5/</ref> As a result, Fiji will play their home game against [[Scotland national rugby union team|Scotland]] away at [[Murrayfield Stadium|Murrayfield]] while [[Wales national rugby union team|Wales]] and [[England national rugby union team|England]] opted to host Fiji at [[Cardiff City F.C.]]'s [[Cardiff City Stadium]] and [[Everton F.C.]]'s [[Bramley-Moore Dock Stadium]] respectively.<ref>https://www.bbc.co.uk/sport/rugby-union/articles/cedzv00vdpdo</ref> Notably Australia hosted a fixture at the [[Perth Rectangular Stadium]] with a capacity below the stated minimum at only 20,500.\n\n===Japan v Ireland game===\nOn 22 April, it was announced that the match between Japan and Ireland would be played at [[Newcastle International Sports Centre ]] in [[Newcastle, New South Wales|Newcastle]], Australia.<ref>{{cite web |title=Japan to play Ireland in Australia as part of rugby’s Nations Championship in July |url=https://apnews.com/article/japan-ireland-rugby-australia-nations-championship-5f1be4c947d004b94e7c4646fad5cbaf |publisher=[[Associated Press]] |date=22 April 2026}}</ref> Originally scheduled as a home match for Japan, the match was moved due to travel concerns raised by Ireland. Japan head coach [[Eddie Jones (rugby union)|Eddie Jones]] was heavily critical of the decision, saying on the Rugby Unity podcast, \"You know why we're playing Ireland in Newcastle? Ireland have all the power at World Rugby. So we have to play our home game, that should be in Tokyo, in Australia to make sure Ireland don’t have to travel too much - let’s be frank about it. We have to just suck it up and that’s what happens when you’re not a major political power at the table.\"<ref>{{cite web |title=Eddie Jones: Irish 'power' led to Japan fixture switch |url=https://www.rte.ie/sport/rugby/2026/0428/1570715-jones-irish-power-led-to-japan-fixture-switch/ |publisher=[[RTE]] |date=28 April 2026}}</ref> [[France national rugby union team|France]] head coach [[Fabien Galthié]] also criticized the decision, stating before their match against Japan on 18 July, \"The only flaw I'd find in this competition, it's that I don't understand why Ireland is facing Japan here (in Australia).\"<ref>{{cite web |last1=Corée |first1=Adrien |title=Fabien Galthié s'interroge sur les lieux de certaines affiches du Championnat des nations : « Il y a toujours des arrangements qui me surprennent » |url=https://www.lequipe.fr/Rugby/Actualites/Fabien-galthie-s-interroge-sur-les-lieux-de-certaines-affiches-du-championnat-des-nations-il-y-a-toujours-des-arrangements-qui-me-surprennent/1703544 |publisher=[[L'Equipe]] |date=11 July 2026|language=fr}}</ref>\n\n==Championship division: Tables==\nThe below shows the tables for the two conferences.\n\n===Northern Hemisphere===\n{{#invoke:Sports
